  * Corrective action: Add comparator B1-SASC: ±k·RMSE_LOO with k chosen per leave-source-out fold by replaying the random-calibrant protocol on the training systems to reach 1−α average coverage. Add it to Table 2, Table 3, Fig. 2 and the decoy analysis. Rewrite the Significance and Highlight 1 to separate fixed and per-system parametric windows, which under-cover, from residual multiples that happen to over-cover. Mention the ±2·RMSE result in the abstract.
  * Required experiment: For B1-SASC, at n = 10/20/40 and α = 0.05/0.10/0.20 on the 228 RP systems, the 37 HILIC systems and NORMAN random calibrants, report:
- coverage;
- share of systems with coverage < 0.85;
- width;
- interval score;
- decoy elimination at matched retention;
- per-system coverage spread (SD across systems) against the proposed windows and jackknife+.
* **[major] R9-04: No real non-target-screening application with database candidate lists** (Section 3.3 (Metrics, decoys), Section 4.6, Limitations; journal_selection/aca_journal_quality_benchmark.md §3 and Implications 3; confidence: medium)
  * Evidence: Section 3.3 builds decoys as 'up to 30 RepoRT compounds with the same molecular formula'. These are curated, mostly metabolite standards, not candidate lists from structure databases. The Limitations section says: 'no prospective, wet-laboratory NTS study was performed'. The ACA benchmark shows that 63 % of accepted papers apply the method to real samples. Its design implication 3 reads: 'Add one real NTS case study with confirmed identities'. This was not done. The NORMAN suspects, with known identities on 37 routine methods, would allow such a case study, but they were used only for coverage.
  * Corrective action: Add a realistic candidate-filtering case study on the NORMAN interlaboratory data. Report:
- retention of the true identity;
- median candidate-list reduction per feature;
- change in rank of the true structure when the RT window is combined with an existing MS/MS or CCS score, wherever spectra or CCS are available.
If possible, add one public NTS dataset with level-1 confirmations.
  * Required experiment: For each of the 37 NORMAN methods and each spiked suspect, retrieve all PubChemLite (or PubChem) candidates with the same molecular formula. Featurise and score them with the fold-free base model. Build windows from the laboratory's calibration chemicals with jackknife+, the proposed method, SASC, Gaussian and fixed ±0.5/1/2 min. Report true-identity retention, number of candidates removed per feature (median, IQR), and the top-1 and top-5 rank of the true structure with and without the RT filter.
* **[major] R9-05: Generative-AI disclosure is inadequate for the extent of AI use, and its text is an unadapted template** (Declaration of generative AI (after CRediT); Section 3.5 Implementation; code git history (commit abbf21e); confidence: high)
  * Evidence: The declaration says AI was used 'for literature searching, code development, data analysis and drafting of the text'. It ends: '*(Wording follows the Elsevier policy; the authors must adapt it to the actual use before submission.)*' and keeps the template wording 'author(s)'. Section 3.5 (Implementation) does not mention AI. Elsevier's policy treats AI use in the research process (code, analysis), as distinct from writing assistance, as something to describe in the Methods. The repository history shows that the analysis plan and implementation were committed by an AI agent: commit abbf21e, 'Author: Research Agent <noreply@anthropic.com>', 'Implement conformal RT-window pipeline, tests, pre-registered plan', with Co-Authored-By Claude. An editor would see this as more than 'assistance' and would query it.
  * Corrective action: In Section 3.5, state which steps an AI system performed:
- study design;
- literature search;
- code;
- execution of analyses;
- figure and table generation;
- drafting.
Give the model and version, and describe how humans verified the outputs (unit tests, independent re-runs, line-by-line code review, manual verification of every citation and of the numbers against result files). Rewrite the declaration to match the actual use, without template wording or placeholders. Make sure the CRediT statement matches the human contributions.
  * Required experiment: None. Optionally, have a co-author independently re-implement the core jackknife+ and the monotone map, and reproduce Table 2 (proposed and jackknife+ rows) from raw data. Report this as verification.
* **[moderate] R9-06: Pre-specified hypotheses H3–H5 are not adjudicated; a post hoc RT-span subgroup is headlined in the abstract without a label** (Abstract (Results), Section 3.2 (protocol), Sections 4.1 and 4.3, Section 4.12 (Error analysis); code/EXPERIMENT_PLAN.md; confidence: high)
  * Evidence: EXPERIMENT_PLAN.md defines H1–H5. The manuscript adjudicates only H1 and H2 ('These results support the pre-specified hypotheses … (H1) … (H2)'); 'H3', 'H4' and 'H5' never appear.
- H5(c), robustness to commonly measured calibrants, failed (0.815; Table 4).
- H3, 'improves coverage of hard compound strata (… ionisable compounds …)', is not shown: ionisable vs neutral coverage was 0.904 vs 0.903, with no jackknife+ contrast.
- The efficiency part of H3 failed in HILIC and NORMAN.
- H4 promised a 'substantial fraction' of decoys eliminated; the result is 24 %.
The abstract's 'longer-than-median runs (… −17 %)' rests on an RT-span split at 16.8 min that the plan does not specify. A 'span model' first appears in Addendum F (commit 64f693c, 2026-11-16 19:07, after the manuscript build). After fold adjustment the effect is weak (partial ρ = −0.16) and confounded with the Folberth study (63 systems, median span 25.9 min). Several other abstract numbers also come from post hoc analyses without a label in the abstract: fixed windows, B7, NORMAN and SASC. Finally, the plan was committed in the same commit as the implementation (abbf21e), which the text does not say.
  * Corrective action: Add a short table (main text or SI) that maps H1–H5 to a verdict (supported, partially supported, refuted) with the evidence for each. In the abstract and Section 4.3, label the RT-span split as exploratory or post hoc. Report the RT-span result with the fold-adjusted estimate, and state that the gain is largely confined to two source groups. In the abstract, mark which headline results are post hoc. In Section 3.2, state that the plan was committed together with the pipeline code and that the commit timestamp is not an independent registration.
  * Required experiment: None. Optionally, run a mixed-effects model of the relative interval-score change on log RT span, with source group as a random effect, to replace the median split.
* **[moderate] R9-07: The guarantee is overstated in Highlight 2, the Significance and the cover letter** (Highlights (bullet 2), Abstract (Significance), Cover_Letter.md (Main findings, bullet 2), Section 2.4; confidence: high)
  * Evidence: Section 2.4 states the formal guarantee as 'P{t(x) ∈ C_α(x)} ≥ 1 − 2α'. That is 80 % at α = 0.10. Coverage close to 1−α is empirical ('close to 1−α when the fitted map is stable'), marginal over calibrant draws and compounds, and averaged over systems. Despite this:
- Highlight 2: 'Conformal windows from representative calibrants keep a user-chosen retention rate'.
- Significance: 'Conformal RT windows make the probability of keeping the correct structure a design choice'.
- Cover letter: 'Conformal windows, which carry a distribution-free finite-sample guarantee, retained 90.9 %–91.1 %'.
None of these mentions the 1−2α bound or that the 1−α level is only empirical.
  * Corrective action: In the Abstract Results, add a clause such as '(formal guarantee ≥ 1−2α; empirically ≈ 1−α)'. Rephrase Highlight 2 to something like 'Conformal windows from representative calibrants retained ≈90 % at a nominal 90 %'. In the Significance, say the operating point is 'predictable' rather than 'guaranteed', or state the bound explicitly. Correct the cover letter in the same way.
  * Required experiment: None. Optionally, report the distribution over systems and draws of per-draw coverage, i.e. coverage conditional on a single calibrant set, which is what a single laboratory experiences. Give the 5th percentile at n = 20 and n = 40.
* **[moderate] R9-08: Submission is not technically complete (placeholders), so a technical return is certain** (Final_Manuscript.md front matter, CRediT, competing interests, acknowledgements; Cover_Letter.md; confidence: high)
  * Evidence: Placeholders remain:
- Author block: '[First Author]^a^, [Second Author]^a,\*^', '[Department, Institution, Address, Country]', '[name, e-mail address]'.
- CRediT: '*(Placeholder — to be completed by the authors.)*'.
- Competing interests: '*(To be confirmed by the authors.)*'.
- Acknowledgements/funding: '*(Funding information to be added by the authors …)*'.
- Cover letter: '[Date]', '[To be completed by the authors: names … of independent experts]', '[Corresponding author]'.
All of these also appear in the PDF (lines 3–5).
  * Corrective action: Complete every placeholder. Remove all bracketed or italic author instructions from the build. Add a pre-submission check to the build script that fails if '[', 'Placeholder' or 'To be' tokens survive in the rendered DOCX or PDF.
* **[moderate] R9-09: Excessive length and density for an ACA research article** (Whole manuscript; Section 4 (subsections 4.1–4.15); Fig. 7 vs Table 4; Abstract; confidence: medium)
  * Evidence: The body is 7,551 words, excluding tables and figure captions, in 17 Results subsections. There are 7 figures and 4 tables, and the PDF runs to 39 pages. The Supplementary Material is 18,931 words with 26 tables (26 pages), roughly twice the main text. The abstract is 261 words and contains about 20 numerical values, which makes the key message hard to extract. Fig. 7 and Table 4 partly duplicate each other: both give coverage of the proposed, jackknife+ and Gaussian windows under the same robustness scenarios.
  * Corrective action: Target about 6,000 words and no more than 8 display items:
- Move Computational cost, Error analysis, the hyper-parameter sensitivity part of the Ablation, and most of 4.9 (SMRT base model) to the SI, leaving a sentence each in the main text.
- Merge Fig. 7 into Table 4, or drop one of them.
- Cut the abstract to about 220 words with 6–8 key numbers.
- Use one consistent term: 'P-spline' in the body versus 'smoothing-spline' in the abstract.
* **[minor] R9-10: Highlight 3 presents a textbook property as a finding** (Highlights (bullet 3); confidence: high)
  * Evidence: Highlight 3: 'At least 1/α − 1 calibrants are needed for a finite window'. The authors' own prior-art report calls this 'a direct consequence of conformal theory' (prior_art_novelty_report.md, item 4). Section 2.4 cites it to Angelopoulos & Bates. Highlights should state new results.
  * Corrective action: Replace it with an empirical result, e.g. 'Fixed ±1 min windows kept only 71 % of correct structures on new systems', or 'Replaying a known calibrant-selection rule restored 90 % average coverage'. Keep each highlight within 85 characters; the current ones are 58–82.
* **[minor] R9-11: Earlier audit fix (C06) not propagated to the cover letter; cover letter inconsistent with abstract** (Cover_Letter.md, Main findings bullet 2; confidence: high)
  * Evidence: The claim-evidence audit (C06) changed the abstract range to '90.9 %–91.8 %', with separate HILIC (90.3–91.4 %) and NORMAN (90.4–91.2 %) ranges. Cover_Letter.md still says 'retained 90.9 %–91.1 %, also for HILIC and, with randomly drawn calibrants, for the interlaboratory data', which is the exact wording the audit flagged as misleading.
  * Corrective action: Generate the cover letter numbers from manuscript_facts.py tokens, as is already done for the manuscript, and match the abstract wording.
* **[minor] R9-12: Largest data source (BGC, 66 systems) is not cited; 'further sources' not identified** (Section 3.1, Table 1; Supplementary Table S1; confidence: high)
  * Evidence: Section 3.1: 'The target systems stem from the BGC data set of Harrieder and Witting deposited in RepoRT, … and further sources (Table 1)'. The BGC set (66 systems, 25,745 RTs, the largest fold) has no reference. references.bib contains no Harrieder/Witting dataset entry other than RepoRT itself. The 22 'Other sources' systems are not listed by RepoRT dataset ID in the main text or in Table S1. Elsevier's data-citation policy expects the original data sets to be cited.
  * Corrective action: Cite the original publication(s) or dataset DOI for the BGC systems. Add a supplementary table listing every RepoRT dataset ID used, with its source group, original reference, number of compounds and RT span.
* **[minor] R9-13: Data/code availability for reviewers and licensing are incomplete** (Data availability statement; Section 3.1; confidence: medium)
  * Evidence: Data availability: code and raw results 'are provided as Supplementary Material and will be deposited in a public repository (archived with a DOI) upon acceptance'. No reviewer-accessible link or reserved DOI is given. RepoRT is CC BY-SA 4.0 (share-alike), but the licence of the redistributed derived files (cleaned per-system RT tables, features, per-draw outputs) and of the code is not stated. The NORMAN data are described only as 'Electronic Supplementary Information … (open access)', with no licence or version.
  * Corrective action: Provide a reserved Zenodo DOI or a private reviewer link now. State the code licence (e.g. MIT or BSD) and that derived RepoRT files are released under CC BY-SA 4.0. Give the exact NORMAN ESI file names and checksums, which the download script already verifies.
* **[minor] R9-14: Graphical abstract uses a truncated axis and omits the residual-rule result** (figures/graphical_abstract.png; code/scripts/make_graphical_abstract.py; confidence: medium)
  * Evidence: In graphical_abstract.png, the right panel's x-axis runs from 0.80 to about 0.93. This visually inflates the difference between 0.885 (Gaussian) and 0.911 (Proposed). Only five methods are shown, so the ±2·RMSE window (0.919, Table 2) and the fixed windows (0.536–0.849) are absent. A reader therefore sees the proposed method as uniquely valid.
  * Corrective action: Either start the axis at a clearly marked broken or zero baseline, or show the coverage gap relative to nominal. Include fixed ±1 min and ±2·RMSE as context, or replace the panel with a retention-versus-decoy-elimination schematic that shows the known operating point.

Verified previous fixes: C06 (abstract coverage range): fixed in the abstract (90.9–91.8 % plus separate HILIC and NORMAN ranges, checked against Table S21). Not carried over to Cover_Letter.md (see R9-11).; C01/C02 (highlights): reworded to 'from representative calibrants' and 'corrected on average'. Verified in the Highlights block and the PDF. Highlight 2 still overstates the formal guarantee (see R9-07).; C54 (heteroscedasticity mechanism): now marked 'plausible but untested' in Section 4.3, line 159. Verified.; C90 (ionisation mechanism in the error analysis): marked 'not tested directly', with ionisable vs neutral coverage (0.904 vs 0.903) reported. Verified.; C94 (memory attribution): now reads 'peak resident memory of the benchmark process, including base-model training, 2660 MB'. Verified.; C22 (target-system information): Section 3.2 now says the difficulty model also used the system's reported chromatographic metadata. Verified.; C09 (required inputs in the Significance): now lists calibrant structures and RTs, routine metadata and candidate structures. Verified. The pre-trained models are still not mentioned, which is acceptable.; C81 (NORMAN missing descriptors): softened to 'unlikely to be the main reason', with the count of 32 PredRet systems added. Verified at line 211.
